The children had been drawing, tried 'I spy' for the 50th time and there was nothing of interest on the radio. Bored with reading and parked in a tight space in a heavy congested area, we were all trapped! For the long haul! I tried to read my Chemistry notes. 1 day until the exam and I was behind on my studies. A familiar tune played on the radio. Time ticked by. Slowly. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. Not driving and minding the kids in a confined space. On a hot day. The kids wanted to be outside, playing. Mum was taking a long time. Seconds turned to minutes, 10, 20, 30 minutes passed. The children had been drawing, tried 'I spy' for the 50th time and there was nothing of interest on the radio. Bored with reading and parked in a tight space in a heavy congested area, we were all trapped! For the long haul! I tried to read my Chemistry notes. 1 day until the exam and I was behind on my studies. A familiar tune played on the radio. Time ticked by. Slowly. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. I had never lashed out like that. And I never would again. I apologised. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. I had never lashed out like that. And I never would again. I apologised. But I had slapped my sibling. I should have been above this. But I wasn't. The children had been drawing, tried 'I spy' for the 50th time and there was nothing of interest on the radio. Bored with reading and parked in a tight space in a heavy congested area, we were all trapped! For the long haul! I tried to read my Chemistry notes. 1 day until the exam and I was behind on my studies. A familiar tune played on the radio. Time ticked by. Slowly. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. I had never lashed out like that. And I never would again. I apologised. But I had slapped my sibling. I should have been above this. But I wasn't. I wasn't perfect. Who is? There were three younger children in the car and they were becoming restless, bored, mischievous! My Mum had left us in the car for the 5th time that day - really we should have just organised things better and had someone else look after the kids and let me do the driving. Not driving and minding the kids in a confined space. On a hot day. The kids wanted to be outside, playing. Mum was taking a long time. Seconds turned to minutes, 10, 20, 30 minutes passed. The children had been drawing, tried 'I spy' for the 50th time and there was nothing of interest on the radio. Bored with reading and parked in a tight space in a heavy congested area, we were all trapped! For the long haul! I tried to read my Chemistry notes. 1 day until the exam and I was behind on my studies. A familiar tune played on the radio. Time ticked by. Slowly. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. I had never lashed out like that. And I never would again. I apologised. But I had slapped my sibling. I should have been above this. But I wasn't. I wasn't perfect. Who is? Since that day I have always remembered the feeling. I now have 4 children of my own and I have never hit them. I have since talked to my siblings about that time and they say they can't really remember it. MMMmmm hopefully not. Bored with reading and parked in a tight space in a heavy congested area, we were all trapped! For the long haul! I tried to read my Chemistry notes. 1 day until the exam and I was behind on my studies. A familiar tune played on the radio. Time ticked by. Slowly. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. The children had been drawing, tried 'I spy' for the 50th time and there was nothing of interest on the radio. Bored with reading and parked in a tight space in a heavy congested area, we were all trapped! For the long haul! I tried to read my Chemistry notes. 1 day until the exam and I was behind on my studies. A familiar tune played on the radio. Time ticked by. Slowly. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. One slap on the leg. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. The shock. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. I had never lashed out like that. And I never would again. I apologised. A lot. But I had slapped my sibling. I should have been above this. But I wasn't. I wasn't perfect. But I will never make that mistake again. Since that day I have always remembered the feeling. I now have 4 children of my own and I have never hit them. I have since talked to my siblings about that time and they say they can't really remember it. MMMmmm hopefully not. I do hope that is the case. I have always prided myself on being calm, collected and fun around my siblings. Their big sister. Their big Auntie. On this occasion I had a lot going on. I think I was in the middle of my senior exams. I'd been driving the family about, out in the car and the errands where taking a lot longer than planned. There were three younger children in the car and they were becoming restless, bored, mischievous! My Mum had left us in the car for the 5th time that day - really we should have just organised things better and had someone else look after the kids and let me do the driving. Not driving and minding the kids in a confined space. On a hot day. The kids wanted to be outside, playing. Mum was taking a long time. Seconds turned to minutes, 10, 20, 30 minutes passed. The children had been drawing, tried 'I spy' for the 50th time and there was nothing of interest on the radio. Bored with reading and parked in a tight space in a heavy congested area, we were all trapped! For the long haul! I tried to read my Chemistry notes. 1 day until the exam and I was behind on my studies. A familiar tune played on the radio. Time ticked by. Slowly. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. One slap on the leg. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. The shock. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. I had never lashed out like that. And I never would again. I apologised. A lot. But I had slapped my sibling. I should have been above this. But I wasn't. I wasn't perfect. But I will never make that mistake again. Since that day I have always remembered the feeling. I now have 4 children of my own and I have never hit them. Bored with reading and parked in a tight space in a heavy congested area, we were all trapped! For the long haul! I tried to read my Chemistry notes. 1 day until the exam and I was behind on my studies. A familiar tune played on the radio. Time ticked by. Slowly. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. One slap on the leg. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. The shock. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. I had never lashed out like that. And I never would again. I apologised. A lot. But I had slapped my sibling. I should have been above this. But I wasn't. I wasn't perfect. But I will never make that mistake again. Since that day I have always remembered the feeling. I now have 4 children of my own and I have never hit them. I have since talked to my siblings about that time and they say they can't really remember it. MMMmmm hopefully not. I do hope that is the case. I now better understand how to keep myself regulated. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. One slap on the leg. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. The shock. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. I had never lashed out like that. And I never would again. I apologised. A lot. I felt sick to the pit of my stomach.But I had slapped my sibling. I should have been above this. But I wasn't. I wasn't perfect. But I will never make that mistake again. Since that day I have always remembered the feeling. I now have 4 children of my own and I have never hit them. I have since talked to my siblings about that time and they say they can't really remember it. MMMmmm hopefully not. I do hope that is the case. I now better understand how to keep myself regulated. I'm a grown up now. I have always prided myself on being calm, collected and fun around my siblings. Their big sister. Their big Auntie. On this occasion I had a lot going on. I think I was in the middle of my senior exams. I'd been driving the family about, out in the car and the errands where taking a lot longer than planned. There were three younger children in the car and they were becoming restless, bored, mischievous! My Mum had left us in the car for the 5th time that day - really we should have just organised things better and had someone else look after the kids and let me do the driving. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. One slap on the leg. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. The shock. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. I had never lashed out like that. And I never would again. I apologised. A lot. I felt sick to the pit of my stomach. I had slapped my sibling. I should have been above this. But I wasn't. I wasn't perfect. But I will never make that mistake again. Since that day I have always remembered the feeling. I now have 4 children of my own and I have never hit them. I have since talked to my siblings about that time and they say they can't really remember it. MMMmmm hopefully not. I do hope that is the case. I now better understand how to keep myself regulated. I'm a grown up now. But it was a hard lesson and I learnt from it immediately. My Mum had left us in the car for the 5th time that day - really we should have just organised things better and had someone else look after the kids and let me do the driving. Not driving and minding the kids in a confined space. On a hot day. The kids wanted to be outside, playing. Mum was taking a long time. Seconds turned to minutes, 10, 20, 30 minutes passed. The children had been drawing, tried 'I spy' for the 50th time and there was nothing of interest on the radio. Bored with reading and parked in a tight space in a heavy congested area, we were all trapped! For the long haul! I tried to read my Chemistry notes. 1 day until the exam and I was behind on my studies. A familiar tune played on the radio. Time ticked by. Slowly. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. One slap on the leg. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. The shock. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. I had never lashed out like that. And I never would again. I apologised. A lot. I felt sick to the pit of my stomach. I had slapped my sibling. I should have been above this. But I wasn't. I wasn't perfect. But I will never make that mistake again. Since that day I have always remembered the feeling. I now have 4 children of my own and I have never hit them. I have since talked to my siblings about that time and they say they can't really remember it. MMMmmm hopefully not. I do hope that is the case. I now better understand how to keep myself regulated. I'm a grown up now. But it was a hard lesson and I learnt from it immediately. The impact of my actions where played out right in front of me. And I never would again. I apologised. A lot. I felt sick to the pit of my stomach. I had slapped my sibling. I should have been above this. But I wasn't. I wasn't perfect. But I will never make that mistake again. Since that day I have always remembered the feeling. I now have 4 children of my own and I have never hit them. I have since talked to my siblings about that time and they say they can't really remember it. MMMmmm hopefully not. I do hope that is the case. I now better understand how to keep myself regulated. I'm a grown up now. But it was a hard lesson and I learnt from it immediately. The impact of my actions where played out right in front of me. I have always prided myself on being calm, collected and fun around my siblings. Their big sister. Their big Auntie. On this occasion I had a lot going on. I think I was in the middle of my senior exams. I'd been driving the family about, out in the car and the errands where taking a lot longer than planned. There were three younger children in the car and they were becoming restless, bored, mischievous! My Mum had left us in the car for the 5th time that day - really we should have just organised things better and had someone else look after the kids and let me do the driving. Not driving and minding the kids in a confined space. On a hot day. The kids wanted to be outside, playing. Mum was taking a long time. Seconds turned to minutes, 10, 20, 30 minutes passed. The children had been drawing, tried 'I spy' for the 50th time and there was nothing of interest on the radio. Bored with reading and parked in a tight space in a heavy congested area, we were all trapped! For the long haul! I tried to read my Chemistry notes. 1 day until the exam and I was behind on my studies. A familiar tune played on the radio. Time ticked by. Slowly. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. One slap on the leg. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. The shock. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. I had never lashed out like that. I think I was in the middle of my senior exams. I'd been driving the family about, out in the car and the errands where taking a lot longer than planned. There were three younger children in the car and they were becoming restless, bored, mischievous! My Mum had left us in the car for the 5th time that day - really we should have just organised things better and had someone else look after the kids and let me do the driving. Not driving and minding the kids in a confined space. On a hot day. The kids wanted to be outside, playing. Mum was taking a long time. Seconds turned to minutes, 10, 20, 30 minutes passed. The children had been drawing, tried 'I spy' for the 50th time and there was nothing of interest on the radio. Bored with reading and parked in a tight space in a heavy congested area, we were all trapped! For the long haul! I tried to read my Chemistry notes. 1 day until the exam and I was behind on my studies. A familiar tune played on the radio. Time ticked by. Slowly. A disagreement kicked off about the seats, who was on their seat or edging into the other persons. Oh dear! I calmed the kids and we started another game of I spy. But it didn't last long. One child poked the other, then a kick, then a pinch, then a pop. Full scale brawl by 3 kids in the car. I raised my voice. Nothing they kept going. I tried to pull them apart. No luck. They hollered and shouted and screamed all the while. In the heat and the cramp tiny car I cracked and smacked the 7 year old who was doing to the most damage to the other two kids. One slap on the leg. I instantly regretted it. Instantly. Her wee face. The shock. There was immediate silence in the car. Then crying. I had made her cry. I had never lashed out like that. And I never would again. I apologised. A lot. I felt sick to the pit of my stomach. I had slapped my sibling. I should have been above this. But I wasn't. I wasn't perfect. But I will never make that mistake again. Since that day I have always remembered the feeling. I now have 4 children of my own and I have never hit them. I have since talked to my siblings about that time and they say they can't really remember it. MMMmmm hopefully not. I do hope that is the case. I now better understand how to keep myself regulated. I'm a grown up now. But it was a hard lesson and I learnt from it immediately. The impact of my actions where played out right in front of me. I do everything I can now to listen to others and myself. How would I redo that day? Probably have got the kids out of the car and went for a short walk.
